People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. world.\n  manifestation: I keep thinking I can't trust my own judgment anymore, like if I miss one detail on a schedule or a machine setting, somebody else is going to get hurt because of me.\n- key: S075\n  name: Social_Detachment\n  description: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.\n  manifestation: At home I sit through family meals like I'm behind glass, hearing people talk and laugh but feeling like I don't really belong in the room with them.\n- key: S077\n  name: Irritability_Aggression\n  description: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) It’s been pretty much like this since then.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable before the accident?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I still do some of the things I used to, like the Sunday stuff with my wife, but it doesn’t feel the same. It just feels kind of empty.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year and seven months where you felt really down or depressed, separate from the general flat feeling you described?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t say there was a separate stretch where I felt depressed in a different way. It’s more been this same flat, disconnected feeling most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you still have interest in activities or have you lost interest in things you used to like?\n\nPatient: I’ve lost a lot of interest, yeah. I still go along with some things, like the Sunday routines with my wife, but I don’t really feel happy or close the way I used to. Even when something goes well at work, I just don’t get that good feeling from it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt extremely sad or depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the sense of a clear period where I was extremely sad. It’s more like I’ve felt flat and disconnected most of the time, rather than having a separate depressed spell.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. hot metal can throw me right back to seeing him on the floor and hearing everyone yelling for help.\n- key: S070\n  name: Trauma_Avoidance\n  description: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.\n  manifestation: I avoid that production line whenever I can, send someone else to walk it, and shut down conversations when people start talking about the rollout or the investigation.\n- key: S071\n  name: Trauma_Related_Amnesia\n  description: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).\n  manifestation: I can remember the meeting before the failure and the ambulance leaving afterward, but the actual few minutes where I was giving directions and trying to help are broken up and partly just gone.\n- key: S072\n  name: Negative_Beliefs\n  description: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.\n  manifestation: I keep thinking I can't trust my own judgment anymore, like if I miss one detail on a schedule or a machine setting, somebody else is going to get hurt because of me.\n- key: S075\n  name: Social_Detachment\n  description: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.\n  manifestation: At home I sit through family meals like I'm behind glass, hearing people talk and laugh but feeling like I don't really belong in the room with them.\n- key: S077\n  name: Irritability_Aggression\n  description: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) at someone before I even knew I was angry.\n- key: S079\n  name: Hypervigilance\n  description: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.\n  manifestation: On the floor I'm constantly checking gauges, exits, people's hands, and where the emergency stops are, and even in meetings I pick a seat where I can see the door.\n- key: S080\n  name: Exaggerated_Startle_Response\n  description: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.\n  manifestation: If a forklift backs up, a door bangs, or a plant alarm chirps, my whole body jumps like something is exploding, and it can take me several minutes before my hands stop shaking.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I've had to hand off floor responsibilities I used to manage myself, and I lost my composure during a regional director interview badly enough that I walked out. At home, my marriage feels strained because I'm present physically but not really participating in our life together.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 7 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on since the retrofit accident about 1 year and 7 months ago.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 47 / manager / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S076\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed. My weight seems about the same, maybe a little up or down here and there, but nothing significant.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other periods in your life before the accident where you felt similarly flat and disconnected?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Before the accident I had normal ups and downs, but I could still feel happy, feel close to my wife, enjoy our Sunday routines, and feel good when something went well at work. It just feels kind of empty.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year and seven months where you felt really down or depressed, separate from the general flat feeling you described?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t say there was a separate stretch where I felt depressed in a different way. It’s more been this same flat, disconnected feeling most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you still have interest in activities or have you lost interest in things you used to like?\n\nPatient: I’ve lost a lot of interest, yeah. I still go along with some things, like the Sunday routines with my wife, but I don’t really feel happy or close the way I used to. Even when something goes well at work, I just don’t get that good feeling from it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt extremely sad or depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the sense of a clear period where I was extremely sad. It’s more like I’ve felt flat and disconnected most of the time, rather than having a separate depressed spell.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed. My weight seems about the same, maybe a little up or down here and there, but nothing significant.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other periods in your life before the accident where you felt similarly flat and disconnected?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Before the accident I had normal ups and downs, but I could still feel happy, feel close to my wife, enjoy our Sunday routines, and feel good when something went well at work. Since then it’s like that part is just... not there the same way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge since the accident?\n\nPatient: Yes. I feel on edge a lot. On the floor I’m constantly checking the gauges, the exits, people’s hands, where the emergency stops are. Even in meetings, I’ll choose a seat where I can see the door. It’s like I can’t just sit and trust things are okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode of feeling this way in your life before the accident?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had rough patches like anyone, but not feeling like this before the accident.\n\nDoctor: Have you had other times in your life before the accident when you felt depressed or really down for a while?\n\nPatient: Not that I can think of. I mean, I’ve had stressful times before, and bad days, but I don’t remember ever being really down for a stretch like this before the accident.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. hot metal can throw me right back to seeing him on the floor and hearing everyone yelling for help.\n- key: S070\n  name: Trauma_Avoidance\n  description: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.\n  manifestation: I avoid that production line whenever I can, send someone else to walk it, and shut down conversations when people start talking about the rollout or the investigation.\n- key: S071\n  name: Trauma_Related_Amnesia\n  description: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).\n  manifestation: I can remember the meeting before the failure and the ambulance leaving afterward, but the actual few minutes where I was giving directions and world.\n  manifestation: I keep thinking I can't trust my own judgment anymore, like if I miss one detail on a schedule or a machine setting, somebody else is going to get hurt because of me.\n- key: I wouldn’t say there was a separate stretch where I felt depressed in a different way. I still go along with some things, like the Sunday routines with my wife, but I don’t really feel happy or close the way I used to. It’s more like I’ve felt flat and disconnected most of the time, rather than having a separate depressed spell.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed. I don’t remember ever feeling this low or cut off from people before the accident and then having it pass. Even if something goes well at work, I know I should feel good, but I just… don’t really feel happy or close to anyone like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode of feeling this flat and disconnected since the accident?\n\nPatient: Yes. I still go along with some things, like the Sunday routines with my wife, but I don’t really feel happy or close the way I used to. Even when something goes well at work, I just don’t get that good feeling from it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt extremely sad or depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the sense of a clear period where I was extremely sad. It’s more like I’ve felt flat and disconnected most of the time, rather than having a separate depressed spell.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed. My weight seems about the same, maybe a little up or down here and there, but nothing significant.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other periods in your life before the accident where you felt similarly flat and disconnected?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It’s like I can’t just sit and trust things are okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode of feeling this way in your life before the accident?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so.
